ALGORITHMS AND DATA STRUCTURES FOR MEDICAL INFORMATICS

QUESTION BANK - 2000 MULTIPLE-CHOICE QUESTIONS

Based on Lessons 1-23.

LESSON 1: Introduction to Algorithms and Data Structures
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. What is the main purpose of Table or matrix in the context of this lesson?
   A) Represent cohorts in which patients are rows and variables are columns.
   B) Represent connections rather than only isolated values.
   C) Make an abstract algorithm executable by a computer.
   D) Ensure that the procedure can be executed consistently.

2. Which of the following was used as an example of Algorithm?
   A) glucose < 70 -> LOW; glucose > 180 -> HIGH; otherwise IN_RANGE.
   B) A risk class, alert, category label, or prediction.
   C) A hospital application containing a database, user interface and alert logic.
   D) Compare a glucose value with thresholds and return Low, In Range, or High.

3. Which term best matches this property: It should be finite, precise and effective.
   A) Definiteness
   B) Algorithm
   C) Glucose threshold algorithm
   D) Input

4. Which option is NOT correctly associated with Output?
   A) A well-defined algorithm must stop after a limited number of steps.
   B) The result produced by an algorithm after processing the input.
   C) It is generated after the processing steps.
   D) Return the result of the computational procedure.

5. Which statement about Software system is correct?
   A) The same clinical information can be represented using different structures.
   B) It is appropriate when relationships are central to the problem.
   C) It is broader than a single algorithm or program function.
   D) Vague terms such as 'too low' must be replaced by precise conditions.

6. Which example best illustrates Program?
   A) glucose < 70 -> LOW; glucose > 180 -> HIGH; otherwise IN_RANGE.
   B) A Python function that classifies glucose values.
   C) Using an exact threshold such as glucose < 70 mg/dL.
   D) Compare a glucose value with thresholds and return Low, In Range, or High.

7. Which statement does NOT describe or support the role of Input?
   A) Supply the information on which the algorithm operates.
   B) It enters the algorithm before the processing steps.
   C) Data provided to a computational procedure before processing begins.
   D) It is broader than a single algorithm or program function.

8. Which property is correctly associated with Output?
   A) It is a common representation for patient cohorts.
   B) It is appropriate when relationships are central to the problem.
   C) It is generated after the processing steps.
   D) The same clinical information can be represented using different structures.

9. Which term best matches this purpose: Provide a predictable organization that algorithms can store, retrieve and process.
   A) Data structure
   B) Finiteness
   C) Output
   D) Software system

10. Which statement most accurately describes Definiteness?
   A) A concrete implementation of an algorithm in a programming language.
   B) The property that every algorithmic step is clear and unambiguous.
   C) A structured arrangement of values in rows and columns.
   D) A finite sequence of precise instructions used to solve a problem or perform a task.

11. Which of the following is NOT an appropriate statement about Finiteness?
   A) The property that an algorithm eventually terminates.
   B) A well-defined algorithm must stop after a limited number of steps.
   C) Ensure that a procedure does not continue forever.
   D) Different programs can implement the same algorithm.

12. Why is Output used?
   A) Integrate algorithms into a full operational application.
   B) Return the result of the computational procedure.
   C) Ensure that the procedure can be executed consistently.
   D) Ensure that a procedure does not continue forever.

13. Which statement most accurately describes Algorithm?
   A) A way of organizing information in memory or in a system.
   B) A concrete implementation of an algorithm in a programming language.
   C) A finite sequence of precise instructions used to solve a problem or perform a task.
   D) A complete application that may include interface, database, logging, security and users.

14. Which concept is described by the following definition: A finite sequence of precise instructions used to solve a problem or perform a task.
   A) Finiteness
   B) Algorithm
   C) Input
   D) Data structure

15. Which statement about Definiteness is correct?
   A) Vague terms such as 'too low' must be replaced by precise conditions.
   B) Its assumptions and thresholds should be documented.
   C) A well-defined algorithm must stop after a limited number of steps.
   D) It enters the algorithm before the processing steps.

16. Which term best matches this property: The same clinical information can be represented using different structures.
   A) Definiteness
   B) Input
   C) Data structure
   D) Output

17. What is the main purpose of Graph in the context of this lesson?
   A) Return the result of the computational procedure.
   B) Represent connections rather than only isolated values.
   C) Integrate algorithms into a full operational application.
   D) Make an abstract algorithm executable by a computer.

18. Which term matches this description: A concrete implementation of an algorithm in a programming language.

   D) Program

LESSON 2: Pseudocode, Flowcharts and Programming Algorithms
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

88. Which scenario is an example of Validation-first logic?
   A) FOR each glucose IN glucose_values.
   B) Check missing heart rate and implausible values before low/normal/high thresholds.
   C) Visit every glucose value in a list and count values below 70.
   D) IF age >= 18 THEN IF resting_state = TRUE THEN classify heart rate.

89. Which concept is described by the following definition: A loop typically used when the number of repetitions or collection elements is known.
   A) FOR loop
   B) Iteration
   C) Flowchart
   D) Trace table

90. Which role is most directly associated with Selection?
   A) Process repeated measurements or continue until a condition changes.
   B) Make the execution path visible and reveal missing branches.
   C) Represent context-dependent rules such as adult vs pediatric logic.
   D) Allow different actions depending on true or false conditions.

91. A student wants to apply the same operation to each element in a collection.. Which concept is the best match?
   A) Sequence
   B) Pseudocode
   C) FOR loop
   D) Flowchart

92. In Lesson 2, what is the best definition of Pseudocode?
   A) A language-independent representation of algorithmic logic using structured instructions.
   B) A step-by-step record of how variables change while an algorithm executes.
   C) A control structure in which instructions are executed in a fixed order.
   D) A control structure that chooses among alternative paths based on a condition.

93. Select the correct term-definition match for Iteration.
   A) Iteration - A control structure that repeats a block of instructions.
   B) Iteration - A step-by-step record of how variables change while an algorithm executes.
   C) Iteration - A visual representation of control flow using connected decision and action steps.
   D) Iteration - A control structure that chooses among alternative paths based on a condition.

94. Which of the following was used as an example of WHILE loop?
   A) Check missing heart rate and implausible values before low/normal/high thresholds.
   B) WHILE sensor_is_connected read and display a new value.
   C) FOR each glucose IN glucose_values.
   D) Visit every glucose value in a list and count values below 70.

95. Which concept is characterized by the following property: Changing the order can change the result or make the procedure invalid.
   A) FOR loop
   B) WHILE loop
   C) Sequence
   D) Flowchart

96. Which option is NOT correctly associated with Pseudocode?
   A) Describe logic clearly before selecting a programming language.
   B) It should be precise enough to implement but independent of a specific language.
   C) Too much nesting can make an algorithm difficult to read and maintain.
   D) A language-independent representation of algorithmic logic using structured instructions.

97. What is the main purpose of WHILE loop in the context of this lesson?
   A) Find logic errors before or during implementation.
   B) Apply the same operation to each element in a collection.
   C) Repeat when the number of iterations is not known in advance.
   D) Describe logic clearly before selecting a programming language.

98. Which role is most directly associated with Sequence?
   A) Repeat when the number of iterations is not known in advance.
   B) Perform ordered operations one after another.
   C) Represent context-dependent rules such as adult vs pediatric logic.
   D) Apply the same operation to each element in a collection.

99. In Lesson 2, what is the best definition of Sequence?
   A) A control structure that chooses among alternative paths based on a condition.
   B) The design practice of checking missing, implausible or unsupported input before classification.
   C) A control structure in which instructions are executed in a fixed order.
   D) A loop that continues while a condition remains true.

100. Which definition is correctly matched to the term Nested decision?
   A) Nested decision - A conditional statement placed inside another conditional statement.
   B) Nested decision - A language-independent representation of algorithmic logic using structured instructions.
   C) Nested decision - A visual representation of control flow using connected decision and action steps.
   D) Nested decision - A control structure in which instructions are executed in a fixed order.

101. Which definition is correctly matched to the term Flowchart?
   A) Flowchart - A visual representation of control flow using connected decision and action steps.
   B) Flowchart - A control structure in which instructions are executed in a fixed order.
   C) Flowchart - A control structure that repeats a block of instructions.
   D) Flowchart - A loop typically used when the number of repetitions or collection elements is known.

102. Which scenario is an example of Nested decision?
   A) IF age >= 18 THEN IF resting_state = TRUE THEN classify heart rate.
   B) IF heart_rate > 100 THEN RETURN 'high'.
   C) Read weight, read height, compute BMI, print BMI.
   D) Tracking weight, height, BMI and intermediate values across the BMI algorithm.

   A) Sequence
   B) Iteration
   C) Pseudocode
   D) Flowchart

   C) Iteration

LESSON 3: Algorithm Efficiency and Computational Complexity
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

175. Which term best matches this property: Doubling n can increase work by about four times.
   A) O(log n)
   B) Worst-case analysis
   C) O(n^2)
   D) O(1)

176. A student wants to compare the scalability of algorithms without relying only on one measured execution time.. Which concept is the best match?
   A) O(n)
   B) O(n^2)
   C) Time complexity
   D) Big-O notation

177. Which of the following is NOT an appropriate statement about Time complexity?
   A) It focuses on growth with input size.
   B) Constant factors are less important than the dominant growth term.
   C) Compare the scalability of algorithms without relying only on one measured execution time.
   D) A description of how running time grows as input size increases.

178. A student wants to describe algorithms that combine logarithmic levels with linear work per level.. Which concept is the best match?
   A) O(n^2)
   B) O(n log n)
   C) Scalability
   D) O(1)

179. Which option correctly pairs O(n^2) with its definition?
   A) O(n^2) - Linear growth in which work increases in proportion to input size.
   B) O(n^2) - Logarithmic growth in which the problem size is repeatedly reduced by a constant factor.
   C) O(n^2) - Quadratic growth in which work grows approximately with the square of input size.
   D) O(n^2) - A description of how running time grows as input size increases.

180. Which role is most directly associated with O(log n)?
   A) Compare the scalability of algorithms without relying only on one measured execution time.
   B) Describe algorithms that combine logarithmic levels with linear work per level.
   C) Describe highly scalable divide-by-half style searches.
   D) Describe operations whose cost stays roughly constant as input grows.

   A) O(log n)
   B) Worst-case analysis
   C) Time complexity
   D) O(1)

   D) O(n) for a scan through n patient records.

   B) Logarithmic growth in which the problem size is repeatedly reduced by a constant factor.

   D) The ability of an algorithm to remain practical as the amount of data grows.

LESSON 6: Sorting Algorithms
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

436. Which term best matches this purpose: Grow a sorted portion incrementally.
   A) Descending order
   B) Bubble sort
   C) Quicksort
   D) Insertion sort

   A) Place data into a useful order for display, analysis or later algorithms.
   B) Its time complexity is O(n log n).
   C) Rearranging elements according to an ordering key.
   D) The key defines the ordering criterion.

438. Which of the following is NOT an appropriate statement about Sorting key?
   A) The attribute used to determine the order of records.
   B) Specify what comparison the sorting algorithm should perform.
   C) The same records can be sorted differently by changing the key.
   D) Algorithm choice matters more as n increases.

   A) The same records can be sorted differently by changing the key.
   B) The next element should not be smaller than the previous one.
   C) Algorithm choice matters more as n increases.
   D) Its average behavior is efficient and commonly O(n log n).

   A) Bubble sort
   B) Efficient sorting for large data
   C) Sorting
   D) Ascending order

   A) Order patients by waiting time or laboratory values by magnitude.
   B) Repeatedly select the patient with the smallest key among unsorted entries.
   C) Insert a newly considered laboratory value into the correct place.
   D) Sort a large set of patient records by merging sorted halves.

   A) Efficient sorting for large data - Using algorithms with near O(n log n) growth rather than quadratic growth.
   B) Efficient sorting for large data - An ordering from larger to smaller key values.
   C) Efficient sorting for large data - Rearranging elements according to an ordering key.
   D) Efficient sorting for large data - A divide-and-conquer sorting algorithm that partitions elements around a pivot.

   A) Ascending order
   B) Sorting
   C) Bubble sort
   D) Merge sort

   B) Keep sorting practical as biomedical datasets become large.
   C) Provide predictable efficient sorting for large collections.
   D) Grow a sorted portion incrementally.

445. What is the correct name for the concept defined as: Using algorithms with near O(n log n) growth rather than quadratic growth.
   A) Efficient sorting for large data
   B) Sorting key
   C) Quicksort
   D) Merge sort

446. Which definition is correctly matched to the term Ascending order?
   A) Ascending order - An algorithm that repeatedly selects the smallest remaining element and places it in the next position.
   B) Ascending order - A divide-and-conquer sorting algorithm that partitions elements around a pivot.
   C) Ascending order - Using algorithms with near O(n log n) growth rather than quadratic growth.
   D) Ascending order - An ordering from smaller to larger key values.

   A) Selection sort
   B) Ascending order
   C) Efficient sorting for large data
   D) Sorting

LESSON 18: Supervised Machine Learning: Classification and Regression
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1480. Which term matches this description: A linear classification model that transforms a weighted score into a class probability.

   B) Logistic regression
   C) Sigmoid function
   D) Distance metric

   B) It is especially important for distance-based algorithms.
   C) Feature scale can strongly affect the computed distance.
   D) Determine which training samples are nearest in k-NN.

1482. What is the main purpose of Sigmoid function in the context of this lesson?
   A) Perform especially binary classification with interpretable coefficients.
   B) Convert the logistic-regression score into a probability.

LESSON 20: Evaluating Machine Learning Algorithms in Medicine

1654. What is the main purpose of Class imbalance in the context of this lesson?
   A) Measure the reliability of positive predictions.
   B) Measure the ability to recognize the negative class.
   C) Explain why some evaluation metrics and training procedures need special care.
   D) Summarize performance when both positive predictive accuracy and sensitivity matter.

   B) It is especially useful for imbalanced classification.

   D) Preprocessing statistics must be learned from training data only.

   B) A trivial majority-class model can achieve high accuracy.
   C) Each fold serves as validation once.

   C) Each fold serves as validation once.
   D) Preprocessing statistics must be learned from training data only.

   B) F1 score
   C) Accuracy
   D) Validation set

   C) Sensitivity or recall
